[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-18
- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 14:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Juice bars are typically not open at 00:10 on a Friday night, and a 9-hour gap between the juice bar and the office is unusually long without an implied activity."
}
```

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-18
- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Coffee Shop#1920 at 13:00', 'Burger Joint#171 at 14:00', 'Shrine#2783 at 17:30', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-19
- Trajectory: ['Office#1347 at 00:10', 'Juice Bar#172 at 00:20', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Burger Joint#171 at 14:00', 'Gift Shop#443 at 16:00', 'Antique Shop#3 at 18:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Juice bars are typically not open at 00:20, and Japanese restaurants are unlikely to be open at 09:30."
}
```

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-21
- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 12:30', 'Burger Joint#171 at 14:00', 'Gift Shop#443 at 17:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Juice bars are typically not open at 00:10 on a Monday morning."
}

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-26
- Trajectory: ['Office#1347 at 00:10', 'Juice Bar#172 at 00:20', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 14:00', 'Sake Bar#1308 at 23:00', 'Taco Place#1 at 23:40']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]

    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-28
- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 12:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 17:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:

}

    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).

    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Coffee Shop#1920 at 10:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 12:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 14:00', 'Gift Shop#443 at 17:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']

    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-10
- Trajectory: ['Office#1347 at 00:10', 'Juice Bar#172 at 00:20', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 14:00', 'Arcade#640 at 17:00', 'Karaoke Box#33 at 19:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "The trajectory shows a visit to an office at 00:10, which is highly implausible as offices are typically closed at that hour.",
  "critique": "Temporal Logic (Opening Hours)"
}
```

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-13
- Trajectory: ['Juice Bar#172 at 00:10', 'Office#1347 at 09:00', 'Japanese Restaurant#2672 at 09:30', 'Fast Food Restaurant#1224 at 14:00', 'Bar#613 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "analysis": "The trajectory shows a visit to a 'Juice Bar' at 00:10, which is highly unlikely to be open at that hour on a Wednesday.",
  "error_type": "Opening Hours"
}
```
